DATABASE_URL…localhost:5433/knowledge_graphNote port 5433, matching what docker-compose publishes. This once disagreed with drizzle.config.ts, so a setup with no .env migrated one database and ran against another.
PORT3000Also feeds the default INSTANCE_ID.
PIPELINE_MODEfieldfield (geometric) or legacy (3-agent LLM). legacy is the benchmark control arm, not a fallback.
CORS_ORIGINSlocalhost 5173/5174/3000Comma-separated. A UI deployed anywhere else is silently blocked without this.
INSTANCE_ID<hostname>:<port>Must be unique per running process. Two tasks computing the same value reap each other's jobs on every deploy. In ECS/K8s, bind it to the task or pod id.

Models

VariableDefaultPurpose & consequence
LLM_PROVIDERollamaollama | openai. Local-first so the system runs with no key.
OLLAMA_BASE_URLhttp://localhost:11434—
OLLAMA_MODELllama3.2:3bDefault for any role without its own setting.
MODEL_EXTRACT
MODEL_RESOLVE
MODEL_VALIDATE
MODEL_VERBALIZE
MODEL_SUMMARIZE
MODEL_UTILITY		—Per-role model, e.g. qwen2.5:7b or openai:gpt-4o-mini. Measured guidance (invariant 22): pay for extraction, economise on verbalization. The resolved table is printed at startup and served from /health.
OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS—Not read by this code; reported on by the router. Distinct local models are not free to mix — Ollama evicts what it cannot hold resident, so a "better" per-role table can be slower end to end.
OPENAI_API_KEY—Required if any provider is openai — including embeddings, which default to OpenAI independently.
OPENAI_MODELgpt-4o-mini—

Embeddings — the sharpest edge in the configuration

VariableDefaultPurpose & consequence
EMBED_PROVIDERopenaiDefaults to openai independently of LLM_PROVIDER. "I configured Ollama and it still wants an API key" is the single most likely setup failure — which is why /health returns 503 naming exactly that. Also accepts local.
OLLAMA_EMBED_MODELnomic-embed-text768-dimension, matching the schema.
OPENAI_EMBED_MODELtext-embedding-3-small1536-dimension. Changing this is a migration plus a full re-embed, never a config edit (invariant 13).
EMBED_DIMENSIONS—Override for a model not in KNOWN_DIMENSIONS. Without it, an unknown model throws at module load rather than guessing.

Queue & ingestion

VariableDefaultPurpose & consequence
FETCH_CONCURRENCY2Ingest-lane workers (network/CPU: metadata, PDF, parse). Raising it earns arXiv 429s faster than it earns throughput.
PROCESS_CONCURRENCY1Process-lane workers (GPU extraction). >1 on a single GPU buys queueing, not speed — measured. On hosted extraction, raise it.
MAX_PENDING_JOBS500Admission ceiling. Bulk requests beyond it get 429 with the current depth. The obligation created by a durable queue.
MAX_JOB_ATTEMPTS3Claims per job before an interrupted or failing job is failed for good.
JOB_LEASE_TTL_MS120000Lease validity without renewal. Heartbeat is TTL/4, so it takes four missed beats to be reaped.
JOB_POLL_MS1000Claim poll interval, plus random jitter so instances do not synchronise.
ARXIV_MIN_INTERVAL_MS3000Minimum gap between requests to arXiv, as their guidelines ask.
ARXIV_USER_AGENTManifold/1.0 …arXiv throttles anonymous callers far more aggressively.
RESOLUTION_CANDIDATE_LIMIT2000Legacy pipeline only. Nodes shown to the LLM resolver, bounded by prompt size. The field pipeline reads the index and has no window (ch. 13).
RESOLUTION_MERGEguardedexact = only identical names merge (zero false merges). guarded = embedding matches must pass merge-guard.ts. vector = cosine alone — audited unsafe: two distinct papers score 0.87.
RESOLUTION_ANN_K8Neighbours fetched per mention from the HNSW index. More than the resolver consumes, to absorb HNSW's approximate recall — a duplicated entity is permanent damage where a slightly wider read is not.
MAX_JOB_CLAIMS25Total claims before a job is treated as a poison pill. Distinct from MAX_JOB_ATTEMPTS, which counts handler failures — conflating them let a deploy spend the retry budget (ch. 16).
SHUTDOWN_GRACE_MS10000How long SIGTERM waits for in-flight jobs before leaving them to lease recovery. Short by design: extraction takes ~15 min and no supervisor waits that long before SIGKILL.
LLM_UNAVAILABLE_ABORT_AFTER3Consecutive unreachable-model chunk failures before abandoning a paper. Small but not 1: a blip is not an outage.

Timeouts, limits, auth

VariableDefaultPurpose & consequence
LLM_TIMEOUT_MS120000Local inference on CPU can legitimately take a minute for a long chunk.
EMBED_TIMEOUT_MS60000—
PDF_TIMEOUT_MS60000Download deadline.
PDF_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS90000Worker-thread parse deadline. On expiry the worker is terminated and respawned.
METADATA_TIMEOUT_MS45000Deliberately generous — arXiv's export API is genuinely slow (~12 s measured).
HEALTHCHECK_TIMEOUT_MS5000—
MAX_PDF_MB50Hard, streamed ceiling. An oversized document is a deterministic property of the URL and is not retried.
AUTH_MODE—required enforces scoped credentials on every /api route, reads included. Unset means every request is an anonymous admin — reported loudly at startup and in /health.
TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS0Proxies you control. 0 ignores X-Forwarded-For entirely. Getting this wrong when you add a proxy silently reopens rate-limit bypass.
MCP_DOMAINS—Comma-separated domains the MCP server may read. Unset = all. How an operator hands an agent a narrower view than their own.
VITE_API_URLhttp://localhost:3000Web app API base.

Two configurations worth memorising

Fully local, no keys
LLM_PROVIDER=ollama

OLLAMA_MODEL=llama3.2:3b
MODEL_EXTRACT=qwen2.5:7b       # pay here
OLLAMA_EMBED_MODEL=nomic-embed-text

B
Appendix B

Glossary

TermMeaning here
Admission controlRefusing new work at a bound (MAX_PENDING_JOBS) rather than accepting an unbounded backlog. The obligation created by a durable queue.
ANN blockingUsing an index to fetch a handful of plausible candidates before comparing them in detail. The unbuilt fix for entity resolution's candidate window.
BatchOne bulk request, many jobs. Progress is always computed from member jobs, never denormalised.
ClaimAtomically taking ownership of a queued job with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED. Increments attempts.
ChokepointrequireDomain(c, raw, action) — the single function through which every route resolves and authorises a domain.
DomainAn isolated knowledge space with its own ontology. The product's central correctness claim.
Ego layoutFocus-plus-context graph layout where edge direction becomes spatial position.
Embedding spaceprovider:model:dimensions. One per deployment, enforced on write, on comparison, and at startup.
EvidenceRanked, MMR-compressed propositions returned by retrieval. The unit an agent reasons over.
Field modeThe geometric pipeline: embedding resolution, rule validation, PPR retrieval. Versus legacy, the 3-agent LLM control arm.
HNSWHierarchical Navigable Small World — the ANN index. Roughly O(log n) search.
LaneA queue partition by contended resource: ingest (network/CPU) and process (GPU).
LeaseA renewed deadline proving a job's owner is alive. Its absence is the death certificate.
MMRMaximal Marginal Relevance — greedy selection trading relevance against redundancy under a budget.
PermanentJobErrorA handler's signal that retrying cannot change the outcome. Saves a 15-minute GPU retry reaching the same dead end.
Poincaré ballHyperbolic embedding model. Norm ≈ generality, distance ≈ relatedness.
PPRPersonalized PageRank — a random walk with restart on query seeds. Where multi-hop reasoning happens.
PrincipalA credential holder — person or agent — with scopes and domain grants.
PropositionOne atomic factual sentence with its own embedding and the node ids it mentions. The unit of compressed retrieval.
ProvenanceThe sources row linking an edge to a paper, section and span. Written in the same transaction as the edge.
ReaperThe periodic sweep that re-queues jobs whose lease has expired.
RRFReciprocal Rank Fusion — merging ranked lists by position, never by score.
SeedsThe seedK nodes nearest the query vector. Entry points into the graph; their scores become PPR restart weights.
Working setWhat one query actually touched, reported in stats. The observable proof of invariant 12.
C
Appendix C

Reading list

The ideas this system is built on, and where each one enters the code.

WorkEnters atWhat to take from it
HippoRAG — neurobiologically inspired long-term memory for LLMsknowledge-field/ppr.tsThe core move: single-pass PPR over a knowledge graph replaces iterative retrieve→reason loops for multi-hop questions.
GraphRAG (Microsoft) — query-focused summarizationknowledge-field/communities.tsCommunity detection + cached summaries for broad questions. One amortised call instead of N reads.
Nickel & Kiela, 2017 — Poincaré embeddings for hierarchiesknowledge-field/hyperbolic.tsHyperbolic space embeds trees with exponentially less distortion. Norm becomes a learned abstraction level.
Carbonell & Goldstein, 1998 — MMRknowledge-field/compress.tsRelevance minus redundancy, greedily, under a budget. Still the right answer for context assembly.
Cormack et al., 2009 — Reciprocal Rank Fusionknowledge-field/fuse.tsPositions, not scores. Outperforms learned score normalisation and requires no training.
Malkov & Yashunin, 2016 — HNSWpgvector indexesHow the ANN index works, and what m / ef_construction / ef_search actually trade.
Chen et al., 2023 — dense X retrieval (propositions)propositionsAtomic propositions beat chunks as the retrieval unit. The single most under-appreciated choice in this system.
Ge et al., 2023 — ICAE / gist tokensReferenced in compress.tsLearned context compression — the ideal MMR approximates without needing model internals.
Fellegi & Sunter, 1969 — record linkageresolve-embed.tsThe blocking/matching decomposition, and why over-merging is irreversible where fragmenting is not.
Raghavan et al., 2007 — label propagationcommunities.tsNear-linear community detection with no parameters.
Postgres docs — SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKEDqueue/index.tsThe clause that makes a database table a safe multi-consumer queue. Read this before reaching for a broker.
Fruchterman & Reingold, 1991 — force-directed placementweb/lib/layout.tsThe spring/repulsion model, and why k = area/n sets the ideal spacing.
D
Appendix D

Exercises

Reading transfers facts; doing transfers judgement. These are ordered from "understand the system" to "make a call a CTO would make", and every one is answerable from this repository.

Level 1 — understand it

1
Run GET /api/field/retrieve?q=… and explain every field in stats. Then predict how each changes if you double seedK. Check.
2
Trace one proposition from a PDF page to a retrieval result: which chunk, which extraction, which edge, which sources row, which signal surfaced it, why MMR kept it.
3
Find every place a domain filter is applied. There are more than you expect. Which one would you remove to break isolation most subtly?
4
Run pnpm --filter api eval. Explain why keyword beats field (graph only) on the hub family and loses on multi-hop.
5
Enqueue a job, then kill -9 the process before it completes. Restart. Explain — from the jobs row — exactly what happened and when.

Level 2 — change it

1
Add a domain for a literature you know. Ingest three papers. Assess the graph by hand: how many entities are real, how many are run-on noise? You have just done the extraction-quality measurement the system lacks.
2
Sweep MMR's lambda from 0.5 to 0.9 through the harness. Does the current 0.7 survive? Nobody has done this.
3
Sweep SIM_THRESHOLD against gold.ts's ResolutionCases — aliases must merge, distractors must not. Report the value and the shape of the curve.
4
Parallelise the three evidence queries with Promise.all. Measure with bench:retrieval. Was the predicted ~13% real?
5
Write a connector for a structured source you use. If any unit comes back with only text, you have violated invariant 20 — find out why.
6
Wire community summaries into retrieval: route broad questions to summaries by comparing best-community similarity against best-proposition similarity. Measure whether it helps.

Level 3 — judge it

1
You have one week before a buyer's technical diligence. Pick three things from chapter 67 and defend the ordering against the two you did not pick.
2
A customer wants a corpus of 5 million documents. Using chapters 45, 63 and 65, produce a cost and a timeline. Then decide whether to take the deal.
3
An engineer proposes replacing pgvector with a dedicated vector database. Write the response: what evidence would change your mind, and what would you measure first?
4
Design the zero-downtime embedding-model migration (ch. 19). Expand/contract, dual-write, backfill, cutover. What breaks if the backfill is interrupted at 60%?
5
Chapter 16 rejected BullMQ. Argue the other side well enough that someone would act on it — then say precisely what measurement would settle it.
6
The register lists 24 open defects; a stakeholder asks why you shipped with them. Answer in three sentences without being defensive.

Level 4 — the hard one

Design the contradiction model Today, two sources that disagree become two edges with confidence scores, and retrieval may return both — or silently favour whichever ranked higher. For a system whose pitch includes error prevention, that is the wrong behaviour, and agents/repair.ts only detects the structural cases.

Design the full model. It needs to answer, at minimum:
  • What is a contradiction? Same subject and object, incompatible predicate? Incompatible metric values? How do you detect the semantic cases the graph cannot see?
  • Where is it detected — at write time (cheap, incomplete) or at read time (complete, expensive)?
  • What does retrieval return? Both sides with a flag? The more recent? The more cited? Who decides, and is that decision auditable?
  • How does it interact with provenance (both edges have sources), PPR (both conduct mass), and MMR (they are near-duplicates in embedding space, so MMR will actively suppress the second one — which is exactly backwards for a contradiction)?
  • What would you measure to know it works, and what would the gold set look like?
That last MMR interaction is the part most designs miss, and finding it is the point of the exercise. If you can answer all five with reference to the mechanisms in this book, you have understood the system — and you are ready to extend it.

Manifold — The Complete Engineering Book. Written against the repository at apps/api ~15,000 lines TypeScript, 250 tests passing (180 unit + 70 Postgres-backed), tsc --noEmit clean, verified while writing.

Latency figures are measured on a 100,000-entity synthetic corpus via bench:retrieval. Quality figures come from pnpm --filter api eval on a constructed corpus in a lexical embedding space. Every unproven claim in this document is labelled as unproven — chapters 51 and 67 are the complete list, and they are the same list a buyer would be handed.

Companion documents in the repository: README.md (what the system does today) · PLAN.md (readiness assessment and roadmap) · CLAUDE.md (the invariants).
